Table 5: Adaptive and Multi-Cues Mechanisms: An Overview (77 papers).

In 2026, Wang et al. (206) design a Triple Adaptive Multiplexing Network (TAMNet<sup>20</sup>) which used a dedicated adaptive multiplexing strategies for feature extraction, contextual understanding, and prediction generation, respectively. First, a Parallel Attention Residual Block (PARB) mitigates feature distortion via an attention decoupling and keeps feature diversity by adaptively integrating feature streams. Second, a Local Saliency Module (LSM) implicitly infer stable target-background relationships from multi-scale contextual dependencies. Third, the prediction mask is generated via a multi-level feature fusion. Experiments show that TAMNet ofers SOTA performance in addition of generalization capability on public IRSTD datasets.

2. Recurrent Mechanisms: In 2025, Liu et al. (82) design a Recurrent Reusable-Convolution Attention Network (RRCA-Net) which incorporates Reusable-Convolution Block (RuCB) in a recurrent manner without introducing extra parameters. Thus, the high-level information of small targets in the deep layers is maintained and further refined. A Dual Interactive Attention Aggregation Module (DIAAM) allows to promote the mutual enhancement and fusion of refined information. Thus, RRCA-Net achieves high-level feature refinement and improves the correlation of contextual information between adjacent layers. A target characteristic inspired loss function (DpT-k loss) integrates physical and mathematical constraints. Experiments on NUAA-SIRST, IRSTD-1k and DenseSIRST) datasets show that RRCA-Net outperforms previous SOTA algorithms while keeping a small number of parameters. In addition RRCA-Net can be employ as a plug and play module to introduce consistent performance improvement in existing IRSTD methods.

3. Difusion Mechanisms: In 2024, Fan et al. (209) propose a Difusion-based Continuous Feature Representation Network (DCFR-Net) which consists of two branches: Difusion-based Continuous HR Feature Representation (DCHFR) and ISDTD. First, DCHFR employs Implicit Neural Representation (INR) into a conditional denoising difusion model to capture extremely small target contours. Second, ISDTD exploits the shared encoder from DCHFR to obtain the HR feature representation, which is fed into Multiscale Implicit Feature Alignment (MIFA) and Spatial-Frequency Feature Interaction (SFFI). MIFA aggregates diferent-layer features in a resolution-free manner. SFFI provides profound SFFI and global-local receptive field mixture to increase the contrast between infrared targets and intricate backgrounds. Experiment with NUAA-SIRST, IRSTD-1k, and NUDT-SIRST datasets show that DCFR-Net outperforms previous algorithms.

In 2026, Tong et al. (207) propose an Energy Difusion Prior (EDPrior) method. A feature recovery network retains the attention of small targets in deep networks by employing a simple encoding space and a multi-stage decoding space. This scheme reduces redundant skip connections. The curvature separation module exploits the thermal radiation difusion properties of small targets as prior knowledge and extracts the target features via a learnable discrete Fourier transform. By reducing redundancy and incorporating prior knowledge, our network can learn efective target features for the detection task more eficiently and purposefully, rather than relying on complex networks to learn the variable backgrounds in diferent scenes. In an other approach, Rong et al. (208) design an end-to-end infrared background prediction method based on a Conditional Difusion model with an Adaptive blocking (APCD) strategy. The adaptive blocking strategy uses both local and global information and accelerates the inference speed of the difusion model. The multi-scale attention segmentation module allows to suppress background clutter and enhance the target. Experiments show that APCD outperforms PSTNN (383), RIPT (430), ALCNet (312) and AGPCNet.

4. Multi-Branch Mechanisms: In 2024, in the context of dense target detection, Xu et al. (389) design a Dual-Branch Dense Collaborative Network (DB-DCNet) which employs two dedicated detection branches. The first branch minimizes missed detections via target-sensitive feature learning. The second branch reduces false alarms through clutter-aware optimization techniques. Both branches adopt the same densely connected network structure but employ diferent feature extraction modules. The first branch exploits small-sized kernels and hybrid attention mechanisms to segment target edges. The second branch employs convolutions with multiple receptive fields and residual connections to filter out background clutter. The detection results of the two branches are processed by a lightweight feature selection network for feature selection and fusion. Experiments on the NUDT-SIRST, IRSTD-Air, IRSTD-1k, and WideIRSTD-Full datasets show that DB-DCNet provide better results than previous SOTA algorithms.

In 2025, Xiao et al. (39) design a Dual-Branch Contrast-Enhanced U-Net (DBCE U-Net) which employs a Contrast Conv Module. The Contrast Conv Module improves contrast and feature representation through adaptive feature segmentation and convolution operations. The dual-branch combines ResNeSt modules for deep feature extraction and feature enhancement modules for visual perception augmentation. DBCE U-Net exploits gradient information to improve small target features and increase the robustness via a dual-branch structure. Experiments on the NUDT-SIRST dataset show that DBCE U-Net ofers superior detection performance with an IoU and Pd of 94 60% and 99 05%, respectively. In an other work, Li et al. (114) design a Multi-branch Mutualguiding Learning Network (MMLNet<sup>21</sup>) which consists of three branches: edge, positioning, and detection. Each of branch consists of a module from a unique perspective. In the detection branch, a multidimensional lossless encoder optimized via a downsampling strategy and multilevel feature fusion allow to mitigate feature loss. In the positioning branch, a target positioning strategy identifies candidate targets from the image by means of a learnable multi-kernel pattern. In the edge branch, a conventional architecture help the model to capture the target shape. A mutual-guiding fusion module adjust information within and between branches. Experiments show that MMLNet ofers similar performance than previous SOTA algorithms. Xiao et al. (121) develop a Dual-Domain Feature Enhanced Network (D2FENet) which synergistically integrates wavelet transform with CNN operations. It allows to extract feature in both frequency and spatial domains via parallel processing pathways. The frequency-domain branch exploits wavelet transform to capture high-frequency details. The spatial branch uses pixel reorganization for feature fidelity downsampling. Experiments on the NUDT-SIRST dataset show that D2FENet preserves target integrity, suppresses false alarms, and ofers superior performance compared to previous SOTA methods.

5. Multi-Scales/Multi-Levels Mechanisms: In 2023, Ma et al. (400) present a Multiscale Super-Resolution Enhancement and Multilevel Attention Fusion Network (MSMA-Net). First, MSMA-Net determines the rough location of targets through a coarse preliminary screening. Second, the network employs multiscale super-resolution to transform low-resolution feature maps into high-resolution representations. Third, the network uses a multilevel feature fusion attention mechanism to facilitate efective information transmission and fusion in multiscale and multilevel feature representations. Experiments show that MSMA-Net suppresses false alarms and provides better performance when the target present a small size and obscure feature information. In the context of MIRST, Yan et al. (81) develop a Spatio-Temporal Diferential Multiscale Attention Network (STDMANet). First, STDMANet employs a temporal multiscale feature extractor to obtain spatiotemporal features from multiple time scales and then resorted them to the spatial multiscale feature refiner to improve the semantics of the features. Second, a mask-weighted heatmap loss allows to train the network with only center point annotations and balances missing detection and false alarm. Experiments show that STDMANet improve the metric F1 score up to 0.9744, surpassing SOTA algorithms by 0.1682.

In 2024, Liu et al. Liu et al. (40) design a Multi-Scale Head with an UNet architecture (MSHNet<sup>22</sup>) using a Scale and Location Sensitive (SLS) loss to each scale of the predictions. The SLS loss tackle the limitations of previous losses. First, a weight for the IoU loss based on target scales help the detector to separate targets with different scales. Second, a penalty term based on the center points of targets help the detector to localize targets more precisely. MSHNet outperforms model-based methods (IPI (382) (382), NRAM (384), RIPT, PSTNN (383), MSLSTIPT (431)) and data-based methods (MDvsFA (243), ALCNet (312), ISNet (73), DNA-Net (53)).

In 2025, Li et al. (72) propose a Dual Branch Multiscale Spatio-Temporal Network (DBMSTN). First, DBMSTN employs a multiscale spatial feature extraction module that exploits inter-frame diference and saliency feature to highlight small targets at diferent scales and suppress complex backgrounds. Second, a dual-branch spatio-temporal feature extraction module with improved gating unit in convolutional LSTM (ConvLSTM) improve the extraction of motion features to deal with their uncertainty. Third, a fusing module combines multilevel spatio-temporal features. A weighted mean squared error (MSE) loss function is employed with adjustable weights of positive and negative samples to address the data imbalance problem. Experiments show that DBMSTN achieves a F1 score. In an other work, Xu et al. (273) develop an Hybrid-Domain Network (HDNet<sup>23</sup>) with multiscale high-frequency information enhancement. More specifically, HDNet combines frequency-domain features with spatial-domain CNN features to increase the target-background contrast and suppress background interference. HDNet consists of two branches: the spatial-domain branch and the frequency-domain branch. In the spatial domain, a Multiscale Atrous Contrast (MAC) convolution module improves the perception of small. In the frequency domain, a Dynamic High-Pass Filter (DHPF) module computes low-frequency signal energy and dynamically removes specific low-frequency information to capture high-frequency image details. Experiments on the IRSTD-1K, NUAA-SIRST, and NUDT-SIRST datasets show that HDNet outperforms SOTA methods. Zhang et al. (395) provide a Multi-level Multi-patch Multi-receptive Multi-dimensional attention Network (M4Net) to capture information interaction among high-level and low-level features. First, a Multilevel feature extraction module (MFEM) with multilayer vision transformer (ViT) allows to fuse multi-scale features. Second, a Multi-Patch Attention Module (MPAM) and a Multi-Receptive Field Module (MRFM) capture and improve the feature information. Third, a Multi-Dimension Interactive Module (MDIM) connect the attention mechanism on multiscale features to improve the learning ability. Experiments show that M4Net provides better results than RDIAN (202), DNA-Net (53) and UIU-Net (155). Wang et al. (83)) design a Wide-scale Gated Fully Fusion Network (WGFFNet) which employs a classic encoder–decoder structure. First, a Stepped Fusion Block (SFB) embedded in the feature extraction stage allows to capture finer local context across multiple scales during encoding. Along the decoding path, the multi-level features are progressively integrated by a Fully Gated Interaction (FGI) Module. A boundary diference loss optimizes the edge details of targets. Experiments on the SIRST-V2 (126) and IRSTD-1k show that WGFFNet outperforms NRAM (384), PSTNN (383), ALCNet (312), DNA-Net (53), ISNet (73), UIU-Net (155) and PBT (432).

In 2025, Yang et al. (7) design a Pinwheel-shaped Convolution (PConv) and scale-based dynamic loss. PConv is employed instead of standard convolutions in the lower layers of the backbone network because it better aligns with the Gaussian-like spatial distribution of infrared small target, enhances feature extraction, expands the receptive field, and employs only a minimal increase in parameters. Even if several loss functions combine scale and location losses, they do not adequately account for the varying sensitivity of these losses across diferent target scales. To address this problem, a scale-based dynamic (SD) Loss dynamically adjusts the influence of scale and location losses based on target size. Employing PConv and SD Loss into previous SOTA algorithms, experiments on the IRSTD-1K and SIRST-UAVB dataset, show significant performance improvements. Yue et al. (115) propose a Shallow-Deep Synergistic detection Network (SDS-Net<sup>24</sup>) which models multilevel feature representations to enhance both the detection accuracy and computational time. First, a dual-branch architecture separately models the structural characteristics and semantic properties of features keeping shallow spatial details while capturing deep semantic representations. Second, SDSNet employs an adaptive feature fusion module to dynamically model cross-layer feature correlations. Experiments on the NUAA-SIRST, NUDT-SIRST, and IRSTD-1K dataset show that SDS-Net outperforms Top-Hat, WSLCM, IPI (382), ACM (37), ALCNet (312), RDIAN (202), ISTDU-Net (159), IAANet (378), DNA-Net (53), UIU-Net (155), and SCTransNet (285).

In 2025, Zhu et al. (46) design a lightweight Hierarchical nested U-shaped Network (HNU-Net) which improve the traditional dense nested structure, using multiple independent U-shaped sub-networks to reduce the semantic gap between the encoder and the decoder, and avoid the fine-grained information of small targets from being submerged in complex and densely nested structures. More specifically, HNU-Net consists of three modules: 1) an Hierarchical Nested Interaction Module (HNIM) which transmits information between the encoder and the decoder, 2) a Multi-scale Residual Block (MSRB) which extracts the features of the input feature map at multiple scales, and 3) a Channel-wise Spatial Attention (CSA) which combines the information of the encoder and the decoder across features and captures fine-grained information through the multi-head mechanism. Experiments on the NUAA-SIRST, NUDT-SIRST, and WideIRSTD datasets show that HNU-Net reduces the parameter quantity by more than 20% while increasing the average intersection over union (IoU). In an other work, Lu et al. (100) develop a Multi-Scale Context Aggregation Network (MSCA-Net<sup>25</sup>) which improves feature preservation and global-local fusion. MSCA-Net consists of three modules: the Multi-Scale Enhanced Dilated Attention Module (MSEDA), the Positional Convolutional Block Attention Module (PCBAM), and the Channel Aggregation Module (CAB). MSEDA captures contextual information across diferent receptive fields keeping fine-grained features. PCBAM enhances spatia understanding by modeling position-aware dependencies. CAB adaptively aggregates multi-level features across channels to highlight key information. Experiments show that MSCA-Net performs well for strong small target detection performance in complex backgrounds.

In 2025, Chen et al. (401) design a Dynamic Content-Guided Attention Multiscale Feature Aggregation Network (DCGANet). First, a Selective Variable Convolution (SVC) module integrates the advantages of standard convolution, irregular deformable convolution, and multi-rate dilated convolution. SVC expands the receptive field and improves non-local features. Second, a two-stage content-guided attention module focuses to salient regions within the feature maps and determines whether these regions correspond to targets or background interference. Third, an Adaptive Dynamic Feature Fusion (ADFF) module allows DCGANet to adaptively integrate contextual features. Experiments demonstrate that DCGANet outperforms ALCNet (312), AGPCNet (245), SCTransNet (285), SeRankDet (433) and DCGADet.

In 2026, in the category of detection based methods, Liu et al. (101) propose SEF-DETR which refines query initialization and comprises three components: Frequency-guided Patch Screening (FPS), Dynamic Embedding Enhancement (DEE), and Reliability-Consistency-aware Fusion (RCF). FPS exploits the Fourier spectrum of local patches to obtain a targetrelevant density map. DEE enhances multi-scale representations in a target-aware manner. RCF refines object queries by enforcing spatial-frequency consistenc and reliability. Experiments demonstrate that SEF-DETR outperforms detection based method (PConv (7), NS-FPN (346)) and segmentation based methods (ACM (37), ISNet (73), ACLNet, DNA-Net). Chen et al. (144) design a method named IRST-RTDETR<sup>26</sup>. First, a global Contrast Perception-Guided Edge-Adaptive Sharpening preprocessing technique (GCP-TEAS) integrates global contrast and local edge information to improve the boundaries of low-significance targets. Second, a Cross-Stage Edge Feature Enhancement Module (CSE-FEM), a Multi-Scale Multi-Head Self-Attention Fusion module (MSMH-AIFI), and a Gated Edge Feature Fusion module (GEFC3) improve small target detail retention, edge representation, and robustness through edge enhancement, cross-scale modeling, and gated modulation, respectively. Experiments show that IRST-RTDETR achieves F1 score improvements over previous SOTA methods. In an other work, Li et al. (405) design a Multi-Scale Feature Enhancement Network (MSFE-Net). First, a Cascading Feature Fusion Module (CFFM) and a Weighted Dilated Convolutional Pyramid (WDCP) improve shallow texture and deep semantic features, respectively. Second, a Weighted Feature Fusion Enhancement Module (WFFEM) learns diferential and combined features across multiple branches. Finally, a Multi-scale Feature Stairstep-upsampling Fusion Module (MFSFM) refines high-resolution texture and semantic features. Experiments on the NWPU VHR-10 dataset show that MSFE-Net provides 92 8% in mAP50 and 62 6% in mAP75 outperforming methods based on YOLOv6 and YOLOv7.

In 2026, in the category of segmentation based methods, He et al. (102) design an Attention-Guided Multi-scale Feature fusion Network (AGMFNet). First, a Multi-Scale

Receptive Field Expansion module (MRFE) structure allows to integrate non-local context information and broaden the acceptance domain. Second, a dual-branch weighting mechanism increases the performance of the model and reduces the complexity of the model through a dynamic modification of the channel weights. Third, a three-Level Asymmetric Cross layer Fusion (TACF) mechanism allows to achieve the efective integration of the low-level, middle-level and high-level features, and ensure that the model can not only capture the details but also the semantics. The loss function mitigate the imbalance between the background and the target of an infrared image. Experiments show that AGMFNet outperforms ISTDU-Net (159), MTU-Net (284), AG-PCNet, DNA-Net (53), UIU-Net (155), DMFNet (434), and TODENet (435). Zhang et al. (116) develop a Hierarchical Perception Fusion Network (HPFNet). First, a Patch-Wise Context Feature Extraction module (PCFE) employs a Patch Nonlocal Block, convolutional blocks and attention mechanism to capture global–local feature extraction and enhancement Second, a Multi-Level Sparse Cross-Fusion module (MSCF) performs crosslevel feature interaction between encoder and decoder representations allowing fusion between low-level spatial details and high-level semantic cues. A dual Top-K sparsification mechanism filters irrelevant background features allowing the attention mechanism to focus more on the target region. Third, an Eficient Upsampling Module (EUM) combines upsampling with multi-branch dilated convolutions improve feature reconstruction and improve localization accuracy. Experiments show that HPFNet provides similar performance than ISNet and UIU-Net suppresing occasional false alarms and missed detections under challenging conditions. Zhang et al. (127) design a scale-adaptive framework named SAFARI-Net which consists of a scale-adaptive processing pipeline dynamically adjusting strategies based on estimated target characteristics, a frequency-aware feature extraction module with learnable parameters improving target saliency across spectral components, and a progressive memory refinement mechanism keeping representations. A triplepath encoder processes spatial, frequency, and contextual information vie dedicated pathways with bidirectiona refinement for comprehensive enhancement. An adaptive clutter suppression mechanism modulates response based on scale predictions. Experiments show that SAFARI-Net achieves 95 77% IoU on the NUDT-SIRST dataset and 70 17% on the IRSTD-1 dataset with improvements for micro-scale targets. Zhao et al. (398) propose a Morphology-Edge Enhanced Triple-cascaded Network (MEETNet) which uses a triple-cascaded architecture that keeps high resolution and improves information interaction between diferent stages. MEETNet employs an Edge-Detail Enhanced Module (EDEM) and a Detail-Aware Multi-Scale Fusion Module (DMSFM). These modules provide edge-detail enhanced features related to contrast and edge information enhancing target saliency and edge representation. DMSFM employs a multireceptive field mechanism to merge target details with deep semantic insights. Experiments on the NUAA-SIRST and NUDT-SIRST datasets show that MEETNet outperforms previous SOTA methods.

In 2026, in the category of real-time algorithm, Jin et al. (117) design an Eficient Convolution and Multiscale Feature Fusion Network (ECMSFNet<sup>27</sup>) based on convolution and attention-guided Multiscale Feature Weighting and Fusion (MSFWF). First, a Dual-Branch Hybrid Convolution (DBHConv) module extracts in frared small target features. Second a Three-Branch Attention-Guided (TBAG) module improves the input features from both spatial and channel dimensions. Third, a MSFWF module is employed at the added detection head to adaptively weight the features and optimize the feature propagation path. Experimental show that ECMSFNet outperforms previous approaches with a real-time detection speed of 74.63 frames/s. In an other work, Kong et al. (399) propose a Context-Aware and Semantic-Guided Adaptive Filtering Network (CSAFNet<sup>28</sup>). First, a Parallel Self-Awareness Attention (PSAA) exploits physical priors to capture global context and incorporates wavelet transforms to strengthen local detail. Second, a Cross-Semantic Adaptive Filtering Module (CAFM) in feature fusion deeply explores infor mation from shallow features and improves the relative saliency of target representations. Third, a Dynamic Multiscale Spatial Pyramid (DMSSP) module enhances edge precision and segmentation accuracy. Experiments on the NUAA-SIRST and IRSTD-1K datasets show that CSAFNet outperforms previous SOTA methods. In other work, Guo et al. (118) develop a Multiscale Sparse Large-Kernel Network (MSSLNet<sup>29</sup>). A Sparse Large-Kernel Convolution block (SLCB) employs a sparse-dense dual-branch design to boost target detection while keeping critical information flow. A Multiscale Sparse Large-kernel Block (MSSLB) model contextual information for target-background separation improved by a cross-gated module (CGM). A Bidirectional Guided Fusion (BGF) module optimizes cross-layer detail-semantic fusion within skip connections. MSSLNet is deployable with only 0.293 M parameters and 2.99 GFLOPs. Experiments on the NUAA-SIRST and IRSTD-1K datasets show that MSSLNet provides similar performance than SOTA algorithms in both accuracy and eficiency.

In 2026, Zhao et al. (402) design a Multiscale Local Intensity and Gradient Network (MLIGNet) which embeds model-driven priors into a unified convolutional architecture. MLIGNet is based on the Intensity and Gradient Encoding Block which exploits morphological cues by encoding local intensity and gradient responses. A Crossing Fusion Module realizes semantic alignment between heterogeneous features. A Multiscale Fusion Module aggregates cross-scale information to increase robustness against variations in target size and signal-to-noise ratio. A lightweight dilated convolutional enlarge the receptive field without compromising spatial resolution. Experiments on the SIRST and NUDT-SIRST datasets show that MLIGNet ofers a suitable balance between detection accuracy and computational eficiency. In an other work, He et al. (403) develop a Multi-scale Fusion and Attention Mechanism Guidance Network (MFAGNet). First, an Hybrid Dual Encoder (HDE) achieves feature-level denoising while extracting features. Second, a Multi-Scale Expansion Attention Module (MEAM) improves feature extraction ability by combining separable convolutions with channel attention mechanisms. Third, a Pixel-level Attention Module (PAM) allows optimization of features encoded by the encoder. Experiments on the ISDD dataset show that MFAGNet achieves an mIoU of 74 12%, a Precision of 99 33%, and a reduced Fa of 0 0002477. In an other work, Zhang et al. (103) propose a Multi-aspect Feature Extraction and Fusion Network (MFEFNet) which consist of three modules: a Multi-Shape Receptive Field Extraction Module (MSRFEM), a Spatial-Channel-Global-Local Joint Re-extraction Module (SCGLJRM), and a Multi-Scale Cross-Attention Mechanism (MSCAM). MSRFEM exploits dilated deformable convolutions and the Res2Net architecture to capture multi-scale and multi-shape target characteristics as well as locally correlated features. SCGLJRM improves global context modeling across spatial and channel dimensions by aggregating complementary local-global information with a cascade of Local spatial-Local channel (LL), local spatial-Global Channel (GC), and global spatial-Local Channel (LC) sub-modules. MSCAM mitigates the feature fusion imbalance in Transformer-based cross-attention by employing correlation matrix transposition and fusion before integrating with feature maps. Experiments on the SIRST, IRSTD-1K, and NUDT-SIRST datasets show that MFEFNet provides superior detection accuracy compared to several SOTA methods.

In 2026, in the context of ship detection, Wang et al. (99) design a Far-shore-Guided Object-focusing Multiscale network with real-time detection Transformer (FGOM-RTDETR) which use multiscale local and global features. A Feature Grouping Module (FGOM) improves multiscale representation and comprises three components: a Feature Reparameterization (FRep) module, a Coordinate Attention Golden Feature Pyramid Network (CAGoldFPN), and a Multiscale Stacked Network (MuSSNet). FRep addresses the loss of channel information due to the small size of thermal infrared ship targets and the complexity of background features. CAGoldFPN enhances multiscale feature fusion. MuSSNet mitigates issues of high target similarity and the susceptibility of small targets to being overlooked. Experiments show that FGOM-RTDETR provides performance gains: precision increases from 0.921 to 0.942, mAP50 increases from 0.938 to 0.958, and recall increases from 0.923 to 0.934. Li and Gui (404) propose a Multi-Scale Direction-Aware Network (MSDA-Net<sup>30</sup>) which integrates the high-frequency directional features as domain prior knowledge into neural networks. First, a High-Frequency Direction Injection (HFDI) module without trainable parameters injects the high-frequency directional information of the original image into the network. Second, a Multi-Scale Direction-Aware (MSDA) module promotes the full extraction of local relations at diferent scales and the full perception of key features in diferent directions. Third, a Feature Aggregation (FA) structure handle target disappearance in high-level feature maps. Finally, Feature Calibration Fusion (FCF) module avoids feature bias during cross-layer feature fusion. Experiments show that MSDA-Net outperforms RPCA-Net, ALCNet (312), DNA-Net (53), UIU-Net (155), ISNet (73), ILNet, MSHNet (40), MMLNet and SDSNet.

In 2026, Li et al. (6) design a Multi-scale Attention Residual network (MAR-UNet<sup>31</sup>) which is based on a Hierarchical Contextual Enhancement Aggregation (HCEA). It adaptively enhances target features. First, a Multi-Granularity Feature Extraction (MGFE) module integrates standard convolutions, Centered Diferential Convolutions (CDC), and Dilated Convolutions (DC) to capture both local high-frequency gradients and long-range contextual information. Second, a Linear Cross-Channel Attention (LCCA) module models global channel dependencies with linear complexity and dynamically adjust attention weights. Third, a convolutional FeedForward Network (FFN) with 3 × 3 convolutions refines feature boundaries. Experiments on the NUAA-SIRST, NUDT-SIRST, and IRSTD-1K show that MAR-UNet outperforms ACM (37), ALCNet (312), RDIAN (202), FC3-Net (211), DNA-Net (53), UIU-Net (155), IRSAM (168), L2SKNet (214) and SCTransNet (285).

## 4.7. Guidance Mechanisms

The existing guidance mechanisms concern features guidance, motion guidance, saliency guidance, memory guidance, feedback guidance, geometry guidance, ODE guidance and fuzzylogic guidaentrnce. Table 6 shows an overview of these diverse guidance mechanisms.

1. Causal Guidance: Most of the existing methods are target-centered by directly learning the target features from backgrounds. They are facing dificulties in effectively capturing stable features due to weak target signals. To address this problem, Duan et al. (436) design a Causal-guided Hierarchical Anomaly-aware Learning (CHAL) framework. CHAL focuses on background learning and the targets are considered as the anomalies in backgrounds. First, a spatio-temporal neural field models the background evolution patterns from generative perspective. Second, a hierarchical anomaly-aware learning decomposes anomaly discovery. Third, a causal-guiding mechanism allows to block the spurious correlations often caused by background confounders, and enhance true target causality. Experiments show that CHAL outperforms single-frame data-based models and multi-frame data-based models.

2. Prior Guidance: Small target is prone to feature attenuation during downsampling and false-alarm amplification during multilevel feature fusion. To address these issue, Zhang et al. (210) design in 2026 a dual-prior guided network with cross-scale calibration and Pixel-Selective Gated Dusion (PSGF). First, a depth-aware prior module integrates a Radial Flux Consistency Prior (RFCP) to suppress structural clutter. Second, a Local Directional Saliency Prior (LDSP) information enhance the isotropic small targets. Third, a multiscale feature interaction module aligns cross-scale representations through channel calibration and contextual aggregation to avoid cross-scale bias. The PSGF module adaptively balances shallow spatial details and deep semantic features to avoid background interference while keeping target integrity. Experiments on the SIRST, NUDT-SIRST, and IRSTD-1K datasets show that PSGF outperforms several previous methods.

3. Features Guidance: To address the low-contrast between small targets and noisy background in infrared images, Zhang et al. (211) design a FC3-Net<sup>32</sup> by exploring feature compensation and cross-level correlation. More specifically, FC3-Net consists of a Fine-detail guided Multi-level Feature Compensation (F-MFC) module, and a Cross-level Feature Correlation (CFC) module. First, the F-MFC module compensates the information loss of details due to the downsampling layers in CNN by aggregating features from multiple adjacent levels. To address the background noise, the CFC module employs an energy filtering kernel based on the higher-level features with less background noise to filter out the noise in the middle-level features. Finally, it fuses them with the low-level ones to learn a strong target representation. Using them into the encoder-decoder structure, the FC3-Net produces a more accurate target mask with a better shape and details.

In 2024, Lee and Park (278) design a Hybrid Network based on Hierarchical Multipatch Feature Encoder (HMAMFU-Net<sup>33</sup>). The encoder divides the image into multiple small pieces and stacks them hierarchically to extract features. HMAMFU-Net ofers a hybrid encoder structure that combines a convolution-based multiscale encoder to extract local information and a hierarchicalbased multipatch encoder to extract global information by running in parallel. Thee loss function fuses the hierarchical information obtained from each layer. Experiments on the NUDT-SIRST and NUAA-SIRST datasets show that HMAMFU-net outperforms several SOTA algorithms. Guo et al. (57) develop a Location-Guided Dense nested attention Network (LGDNet). LGDNet comprises a Pixel Attention Guided Feature Extraction Module (PAG-FEM), a Channel Attention Guided Feature Fusion Module (CAG-FFM), and a detection module. PAG-FEM employs the DNIM dense nested blocks from the DNA-Net as the backbone, integrating both channel and pixel attention mechanisms. Second, CAG-FFM uses upsampling and convolution operations to align the feature sizes, while using the channel attention mechanism to obtain channel information. Third, these features are fused via stacking, addition, and averaging operations to obtain discriminative features. The detection module empoys eight-connected neighborhood clustering method to obtain the centroid coordinates of the targets for subsequent detection evaluation. Experiments show that LGDNet provides better results than previous SOTA methods.

Table 6: Guidance Mechanisms: An Overview (116 papers).

In 2025, Chen et al. (74) develop a hybrid deep-learning framework that combines dense spatial heatmap supervision with geometry-aware regression (PSHNet). It generates position–scale heatmaps to guide coarse localization, which are refined through sub-pixel ofset and size regression. A Complete IoU (CIoU) loss is employed as a geometric regularization term to enhance alignment between predicted and ground-truth bounding boxes. An Enhanced Low-level Feature Module (ELFM) is incorporated using multi-scale dilated convolutions and channel attention to preserve fine spatial details. Experiments on the NUDT-SIRST and IRSTD-1k datasets show that PSHNet outperforms DNA-Net (53) and ALCNet (312) in IoU, detection probability, and false alarm rate. In an other work, Xu et al. (461) design an infrared small target detection method based on Weak Feature Enhancement and Target Adaptive Proliferation (IRSTD-WFETAP). First, a sparse sampling mechanism and hybrid filtering method capture the complex shapes and edge information of small targets while reducing the loss and shift of scarce features. Second, a multiscale feature enhancement module employs a vertical-horizontal bidirectional attention and a multiscale feature encoding to obtain stable feature interaction channels between the encoder and decoder. Third, a Target Data Self-Adaptive proliferation Strategy (DSAS) allows to address the imbalance of positive and negative samples for generalization and expression capability. A target-background joint loss alleviate the imbalance issue and help the network converge smoothly.

Experiments on the NUAA-SIRST and IRSTD-1k datasets show that IRSTD-WFETAP provides better performance in nIoU, Pd, Fa, and F1-measure compared to previous methods.

In 2025, Liu et al. (55) propose a Prior Guided Network (PGDN-Net) which combines traditional manual features with a deep learning network model. First, three prior features are extracted namely the high-order Riesz transform feature, the compactness and heterogeneity feature (CH), and the corner feature of the structure tensor (ST). Second, these features are input into a dense nested network for guidance with a two-orientation attention aggregation module and a channel and spatial attention module. Each feature ofers their respective guiding roles in diferent depths. Experiments show that PGDN-Net provides better performance in background suppression, target enhancement, probability of detection, and false alarm rate than previous algorithms. Liu et al. (449) develop a lightweight detection network named MDS-DWGANet which comprises a Multi-Directional Sparse Convolution module (MDSC) and a Dynamic Weight Guided Attention module (DWGA). First, MDSC constructs sparse convolution branches in four directions (horizontal, vertical, and two diagonals) to simulate directional gradient responses, and uses a sparse gating mechanism to focus on high-response edge regions, maintaining structural awareness while reducing parameter overhead. Second, a Dynamic Weight Guided Attention module (DWGA) mitigates feature dilution and attention drift by integrating four attention pathways with a dynamic channel allocation mechanism. Practically, DWGA can be considered as a deep generalization of regional contrast enhancement. Experiments on the NUDT-SIRST and IRSTD-1 K datasets show that MDS-DWGANet provides 98 30% in detection probability (Pd) and 93 04% in mean Intersection over Union (mIoU) with 1 4 M parameters.

In 2025, Ma et al. (450) design an Orthogonal Inputs Perception Fusion (OIPF) network to capture the learnable target features in deeper layers without increasing network depth. The orthogonal data pairs through intensity inversion and enhancement are then fed into a dual-input framework to provide incremental information flow. Second, a Relationally Aware Module (RAM) generates spatial weight maps by exploiting the relationships between data pairs across layers. Extensive experiments show that OIPF significantly improves ISTD performance. Yang et al.(453) develop an Attention-Guided Dual Feature Extraction (AGDFE<sup>34</sup>) network which comprises two levels of feature extraction: a multi-scale feature extraction mechanism and a contextual feature extraction mechanism. First, the multi-scale feature extraction mechanism implements the feature extraction operation of the first layer. Thus, the information interaction between neighboring layers is done by fusing and splicing the features of diferent layers. Second, the target attention mechanism enhances the model’s attention to small targets and ofers efective attention allocation for the detection of small infrared targets. Third, the multi-resolution feature interaction mechanism preserves the detail information and improves the ability to perceive small targets. The contextual feature extraction mechanism implements the second-level feature extraction operation background environments. Experiments show that the AGDFE provides 99 81% vs. 87 58% Precision, 98 96% vs. 88 12% Recall, 99 38% vs. 87 85% F-measure and 98 77% vs. 78 33% mIoU on NUDT-SIRST and SIRST Aug datasets, respectively.

In 2025, in the context of generalization in the presence of unseen scenes, Chi et al. (452) develop a contrastinvariant feature extraction framework for Single-domain Generalization (SGIRNet). First, a Contrast Modulation module (CM) addresses the feature domain gap by interleaving enhancement and suppression operations. Second, a Position and Channel Invariant Feature extraction Module (PCIFM) extracts target features that are not afected by contrast changes. SGIRNet is integrated into several existing models to evaluate the performance. Experiments show that SGIRNet improves the detection performance of existing models in the unseen domain providing superior generalization ability.

In 2025, in the context of MIRST, Peng et al. (451) develop a Spatio-Temporal feature fusion networks (STME<sup>35</sup>) which comprises two parallel feature extraction branches. The fist one extracts temporal features across entire video clip. The second one isolates spatial features from a selected key frame. A self-refining module allows to adaptively adjust the extracted temporal features and fusing spatio-temporal features at diferent scales Extensive experiments show that STME reduces floating point operations per second (FLOPs).

In 2025, in the context fo plug and play component, Lu et al. (108) design a Dual Geometry-Guided Experts Network (DGGENet) based on the Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) paradigm with a Target-Background Decoupling Module (TBDM). Specifically, TBDM uses a geometryguided dynamic routing mechanism functioning as an adaptive gating network. Thus, it continuously analyzes input features to dynamically establish two specialized expert groups. The first one is dedicated to target channels and the seoond one is dedicated to background channels. Each group functions as a sub-network specialized for its assigned feature subspace. Using the RPCA principles, the inter-group expert iteration promotes low-rank background and sparse target representations during collaborative feature refinement. Second; a cross fusion module acts as state feedback allowing semantically consistent interaction between the optimized representations. This closed-loop interaction explicitly models target-background correlations. Experiments show that DGGENet) provides superior performance on the NUDT-SIRST dataset and the IRSTD-1K dataset. TBDM is thus a versatile plug-and-play component which improved diverse ISTD frameworks.

In 2026, Liu et al. (47) design a Perceptual Query Guided Network PQGNet. First, a Perceptual Query Supervision Mechanism (PQGNet<sup>36</sup>) employs a perceptual loss to constrain spatial feature learning of each encoder layer. Second, a Perceptual Feature Construction Module (PFCM) captures perceptual features to preserve target localization information. Third, a Perceptual Query Quidance Module (PQGM) employs a cross-attention to guide global and regional feature queries through skip connections, optimizing target feature extraction. A max pooling-wavelet hybrid layer (MWHL) and high-frequency enhanced wavelet layer (HEWL) exploit discrete wavelet transform properties to improve deep semantic representations using shallow high-frequency details. Experiments on the NUDT-SIRST, NUAA-SIRST, and IRSTD-1K datasets demonstrate that PQGNet outperforms SOTA methods with a competitive balance between computational complexity and accuracy.

In 2025, Li et al. (86) propose a Hybrid Mamba–CNN UNet (HMCNet). Its hybrid architecture allows to extract the local features and model the global context. Experiments on IRSTD-1k and MISTD (86) dataset show that HMCNet outperforms RLCM, MPCM, PSTNN and RDIAN (202) with high-speed inference capabilities. Lu and Zhang (10) integrate a visual Mamba module into a lightweight CNN-based network which combines the ability of CNNs to model local features and a visual Mamba module captures long-distance global features. Therefore, this model extract both local and long-distance global feature information. Experiments on the NUAA-SIRST and NUAA-SIRSTv2 datasets show that this model provides better accuracy and lower computational complexity.

In 2025, Li et al. (86) propose a Hybrid Mamba–CNN UNet (HMCNet). Its hybrid architecture allows to extract the local features and model the global context. Experiments on IRSTD-1k and MISTD (86) dataset show that HMCNet outperforms RLCM, MPCM, PSTNN and RDIAN (202) with high-speed inference capabilities. Lu and Zhang (10) integrate a visual Mamba module into a lightweight CNN-based network which combines the ability of CNNs to model local features and a visual Mamba module captures long-distance global features.

Therefore, this model extract both local and long-distance global feature information. Experiments on the NUAA-SIRST and NUAA-SIRSTv2 datasets show that this model provides better accuracy and lower computational complexity.

In 2026, in the context of gradient features, Wang et al. (455) propose a Gradient-Compensation-based Feature Learning Network (GCFLNet) based on a multi-module collaborative design to improve feature representation and fusion. First, an Edge Enhancement Module (EEM) captures fine-grained edge information while suppressing background noise via smoothing operations. Thus, the extracted edge features are embedded into a Global–Local Feature Interaction (GLFI) module. A Multi-Scale Information Compensation (MSIC) module exploits the complementary characteristics of multi-scale features across spatial and channel dimensions, guiding eficient fusion of high-level and low-level information. Experiments on the NUDT and IRSTD-1K datasets show that GCFLNet outperforms outperforms filter-based approaches (Top-Hat, Max–Median), local contrast-based methods (TLLCM, WSLCM), model-based methods (RIPT, IPI), and data-based approaches (UIU-Net (155), DNA-Net (53), RDIAN (202)) in heterogeneous backgrounds. Du et al. (456) design a Dual-Path Edge-Guided Frequency-Aware Network (DEFANet) which allow edge-target collaborative modeling for enhanced feature representation. DEFANet features comprises a main branch for holistic target modeling and an edge branch for boundary transition perception. A two core modules: Frequency-Aware Dual Enhancement Module (FADE) and Edge-Guided Integration Module (EGI) allow interac tion and representation in both branches. First, FADE uses a Frequency-Decoupled Attention Enhancement Mecha nism to enhance both branches in the frequency domain, strengthening holistic modeling in the main branch and boundary representation in the edge branch. Second, EGI exploits a Dual-Path Group-Wise Guidance Mechanism to integrate edge features into the main branch. Experiments show that DEFANet provides similar performance than MDvsFA (243), LAFT, SIRST, and SIATD. Yang and Cui (457) develop an Edge-Shape Enhanced Network (ESE-Net) which enhance edge feature representations to improve target discrimination. First, a Multiscale Spatial Edge Attention (MSEA) module enhances target edges by perceiving directional gradient changes. Second, an Edge Guidance Module (EGM) extracts edge features in the frequency domain through a wavelet transform and performs reversible down sampling, discarding low frequency components before fusing with spatial features. Third, a Multiscale Group Convolution Module (MGCM) is integrated in deep layers to preserve target details and mitigate the risk of small target loss. Experiments on the NUAA-SIRST and IRSTD-1K datasets show that ESE-Net outperforms UIU-Net (155), DNA-Net (53), and RDIAN (202).

In 2026, Xu et al. (458) design a a Native-resolution feature extraction and fusion framework (Na-IRSTD) which employs native-resolution features to keep subtle target cues. An token reduction and selection strategy selects target patches with high accuracy and confidence, boosting the low-level details of the feature while efectively reducing native-resolution patch tokens compared to dense processing. Experiments show that Na-IRSTD outperforms models-based methods (NRAM (384), PSTNN (383)) and data based methods (ACM (37), DNA-Net (53), ISNet (73), UIU-Net (155), RP-CANet (529), IRMamba, SAIST (249)). Li et al. (454) propose a Spatial-Frequency Feature Learning Network (SFLNet) which attempt to solve the challenges in small target detection through collaborative modeling of the spatial and frequency domains. First, SFLNet adopts an edge-guided decoder structure that preserves the structural integrity and shape information of small targets during reconstruction. Second, this module employs a spatial–frequency joint learning mechanism that allows complementary and enhanced information exchange between the spatial and frequency domains. Experiments show that SFLNet outperforms previous SOTA algorithms in precise localization of targets and preservation of target shapes.

4. Reconstruction Guidance: In 2026, Yan et al. (222) design a target-aware invertible encoder (InvDet<sup>37</sup>) that combines information preservation and target-aware enhancement within a reconstruction-guided detection framework. First, an invertible pathway builds the input from feature latents considering information loss as an optimizable quantity. Second, a Target-Aware Reconstruction Modulation (TARM) module operates only in the inverse path. Third, a Geometry–Content Tolerance Metric (GCTM) focuses on truly informative regions and yields a pixel-wise weight map that regularizes the reconstruction branch.

5. Semantic Guidance: Most of deep neural networks methods sufers from semantic degradation when transferring high-level features from deep to shallow layers, limiting the precise localization of small targets. To address this problem, several researches propose to employ semantic guidance. In 2024, Ma et al. (484) develop a small target detection network based on High- and Low-Frequency Semantic Reconstruction (HLSR-net) to suppress background clutter interference and to extract sparse features of targets. First, high-frequency target and low-frequency background features are extracted separately via fast Fourier frequency transformation. Second, target enhancement and background suppression are carried out through the designed high- and low-frequency reconstruction networks, respectively. Third, a fusion output strategy provides the detection of small targets. Experiment show HLSR-net achieves better performance with an accuracy improvement of 6%. Xiao et al. (486)) design a Background-Aware Feature Exchange Network (BAFE-Net<sup>38</sup>) which is a multi-task architecture that jointly addresses target detection and background semantic segmentation. BAFE-Net employs a dynamic cross-task feature hard-exchange mechanism allowing efective exchange of target and background semantics between the two tasks. Experiments on the DenseSIRST dataset show that BAFE-Net outperforms ACM (37), UIU-Net (155), MTU-Net (284), DNA-Net (53), RDIAN (202), URAnet (367), ABCNet (530) and SeRankDet (433). Hu et al. (531) design a scene semantic prior-assisted infrared ship detection using multitask perception network (SMPISD-MTPNet<sup>39</sup>). This network is based on multitask perception: one task predict targets, and the other focuses on scene perception to suppress false alarms caused by background interference. A Scene Semantic Extractor (SSE) guide the network according to the features extracted based on expert knowledge and the gradient-based module. A data augmentation is employed and a training trick named soft fine-tuning enhance the network’s generalization and suppress the distortion due to the augmentation process. Experiments on the IRSDSS datasets show that SMPISD-MTPNet outperforms previous SOTA methods.

In 2025, Zhuang et al. (77) develop a Temporal-Semantic Interaction Network (TSI-Net) for multi-frame infrared small target detection. TSI-Net employs a Temporal Feature Extraction Module (TFEM) which allows to capture the motion characteristics of targets in consecutive frames. To tackle insuficient feature interaction, a Feature Interaction-Aware Module (FIAM) based on a multi-scale semantic interaction Transformer allows robust crosslevel feature fusion. Finally, a multi-task weighted hybrid loss function allows to balance classification accuracy, region alignment, and pixel-level boundary delineation. Experiments on the NUDT-MIRSTD and SIATD datasets show that TSI-Net outperforms ALCNet (312), DNA-Net (53), UIU-Net (155), MSLSTIPT (431), ASTTV-NTLA (532), IMNN-LWEC (532) and STDMANet (81). Shi et al. (485) propose an Auxiliary Metadata Driven Infrared Small Target Detector (AuxDet<sup>40</sup>) to incorporate metadata into the IRSTD paradigm for scene-aware optimization. Using a high-dimensional fusion module, AuxDet dynamically employs metadata semantics with visual features, guiding adaptive representation learning for each individual sample. A lightweight prior-initialized enhancement module using 1D convolutional blocks refine fused features and recover fine-grained target cues. Experiments on the WideIRSTD-Full dataset demonstrate that AuxDet outperforms ALCNet (312).

In 2026, Ji et al. (104) propose a Three-Stage Networks (TSNet) using an encoder-decoder architecture with an upsampling-free subnetwork to fuse spatial and semantic information. The first two stages extracts semantic features viz a Patch-based Multi-scale Feature Enhancement Module (PMFEM). The final stage uses a sub-network without upsampling to avoid spatial information loss that occurs in traditional downsampling processes. This sub-network consists of a Cross-feature Fusion Block (CFB) combined with 2D Selective Scan (SS2D) to capture spatial information while employing high-level semantic features. Experiments on the SIRST, NUDT-SIRST, and IRSTD-1k datasets show that the TSNet outperforms ACM (37), ALCNet (312), DNA-Net (53), UIU-Net (155), TMNet, RDIAN (202) and AGPCNet (245). In an other work, Wu et al. (481) design a Semantic-Detail Collaborative Network (SemDetNet). First, a background-aware global semantic module allows to enhance the discriminative representation of non-target regions through reverse compensation in multi-level downsampling. Second, a detail compensation module exploits shallow multi-granularity details. This module further exploits coarse-grained prediction masks to guide deep features, enabling semantic reconstruction and edge refinement. Experiments show that SemDetNet provides better results than HCFNet, MSHNet (40), MTU-Net (284) and DNA-Net (53). In an other work, Zhang et al. (482) propose a lightweight detection framework incorporating Frequency-aware and Semantic Guidance networks (FSGNet<sup>41</sup>). First, a multi-directional interactive attention module captures fine-grained and directional features. Second, a multi-scale frequency-aware module exploits Fast Fourier transform to preserve salient target structures. Third, a global pooling module captures high-level semantic information.Then, it is upsampled and propagated to each decoder stage via a global semantic guidance flows allowing semantic consistency and precise localization across scales. Experiments on the NU-AASIRST, IRSTD-1K, NUDT-SIRST, and SIRSTAUG datasets show that outperforms ACM (202), RDIAN (202), AGPCNet (245), DNA-Net (53), UIU-Net (155), RPCA-Net (533), MSHNet (40) and L2SKNet (214). Liao an Yin (483) design Camouflage-aware Counter-Distraction Network (CCDNet). First, a backbone with Weighted Multi-branch Perceptrons (WMPs), aggregates self-conditioned multi-level features to represent the target and background. Second, a Aggregation-and-Refinement Fusion Neck (ARFN) to refine structures/semantics from shallow/deep features maps, and bidirectionally obtain the relations between the targets and the backgrounds. Third, a Contrastive-aided Distractor Discriminator (CaDD), enforces adaptive similarity computation both locally and globally between the real targets and the backgrounds to discriminate distractors, so as to reduce the false alarm rate. Sang et al. (487) propose an Edge-Target Deep

Semantic Consistency (ET-DSC<sup>42</sup>) semantic adaptive balancing framework. In encoder, shallow-layer modeling and gated fusion enhance target boundaries. In decoder, semantic consistency constraints keep real boundaries and suppress false edges. A semantic allocation mechanism between shallow and deep layers allows cooperative optimization between edge compensation and semantic preservation. Experiments on the NUDT-SIRST and NUAA-SIRST datasets show that ET-DSC outperforms ACM (37), UNet++ (59), HCFNet, DNA-Net (53), MTU-Net (284) and MSHNet (40). Zhang et al. (106) propose a Semantic Density-Guided ResNet (SDG-ResNet). It is a plug-in backbone that attaches a lightweight semantic density head to the deepest stage and injects the predicted density map into intermediate layers through Semantic Density-Guided Refine (SDGR) blocks with residual spatial gating.

6. Motion Guidance: Multi-frame approaches learn motion implicitly through deep neural networks but often require additional motion supervision or explicit alignment modules In 2026, Liu et al. (146) propose Motion Integration DETR (MI-DETR<sup>43</sup>) bio-inspired with a dual-pathway detector which processes one infrared frame per time step while modeling motion. First, a Retina-inspired Cellular Automaton (RCA) converts raw frame sequences into a motion map defined on the same pixel grid as the appearance image. RPCA allows parvocellular-like appearance and magnocellular-like motion pathways to be supervised by a single set of bounding boxes without extra motion labels or alignment operations. Second, a Parvocellular-Magnocellular Interconnection (PMI) Block allows a bidirectional feature interaction between the two pathways giving a biologically motivated intermediate interconnection mechanism. Third, a RT-DETR decoder employs features from the two pathways to provide detec tion results. Experiments on the ITSDT-15K, IRDST-H, and DAUB-R datasets show that MI-DETR outperforms single frame detection methods (SANet (200), AGPCNet (245), RDIAN (202), DNA-Net (53), RPCA-Net (533), L2SKNet (214)) and multi-frame detection methods (SSTNet (152)). Zhang et al. (274) design a Motionguided Spatial–Temporal integrated Network (MIST-Net). First, the input strategy encodes the dominant motion characteristics across multiple consecutive frames using series expansion and temporal motion intensity analysis. This strategy allows to build a motion frame that enforces motion information from several frames into one. The motion frame and target frame are then fed into the integrated network to extract motion and spatial features, respectively. Second, a Mutual Calibration Module (MCM) refine the motion maps using spatial cues and improves the spatial maps with motion information. Third, a semantic gap between motion and spatial features is obtained by using a Motion-Spatial-Coupled selective state space Module (MSCM). MSCM dynamically adjusts the fusion of motion and spatial information. Experiments on SIRST and ITSDT-15K datasets show that MIST-Net outperforms previous SOTA algorithms. Zhang et al. (275) propose MOCID<sup>44</sup> which is a spatio-temporal backbone with Fourier-inspired Spatio-temporal Attention (FISTA) layers. Each FISTA layer sequentially processes the features from spatial and temporal views to capture clip-level temporal motion context. This context is then embedded into dynamic convolutional kernels for subsequent spatial feature extraction allowing motion difference guidance and generating features. For frame-level feature fusion, a Displacement-aware Mamba Module (DAM) capture detailed frame-to-frame displacement information. DAM employs a Temporal Interpolation and Displacement-aware Scan method to perform spatiotemporal diference-aware displacement modeling. Thus, MOCID capture motion and displacement contexts, Experiments on the IRDST and DAUB datasets how that MOCID provides a superior balance between throughput and performance compared to other methods.

7. Saliency Guidance: Most of the conventional deep neural networks struggle to perceive the sensitivity of small targets and adopt heavy constructions to preserve potential target features ignoring domain-specific insights and sufering from poor explainability. In 2025, Wu et al. (214) attempt to address this problem by revisiting the saliency principles and develop a Learnable Local Saliency Kernel Network (L2SKNet<sup>45</sup>). A learnable Local Saliency Kernel Module (LLSKM) employs the concept of “Center subtracts Neighbors,” guiding the network to capture the saliency features. In addition LLSKM uses a strategic dilation and is refined into a more compact form by factorizing it into two orthogonal 1-D kernels making it lightweight. Experiments show that L2SKNet outperforms previous methods.

8. Physics-Informed Guidance: Conventional SAM-based detectors are based on external prompts and encoders pretrained with natural images. This training limits their practicality when prior knowledge is scarce and targets are extremely small (534). To address this problem, Li et al. (85) design in 2026 a Physic-Informed SAM (PI-SAM) method which embeds infrared physical priors into the encoder. PI-SAM consists of three components. First, a Physics-aware Encoder Adapter (PEA) estimates local luminance contrast and a SNR via center–surround mechanism modulates multilevel features across spatial and channel dimensions. Second, a heatmap-prompt mechanism converts physical cues into non interactive prompt heatmaps and refines predictions through conditionally parameterized convolutions. Third, a Physics-Constrained Loss (PCL) imposes both contrast- and SNR-aware regularization with sparsity and total-variation constraints. Experiments show that PI-SAM substantially improves the SAM baseline, confirming that incorporating physical principles into foundation models improves it suitability in terms of robustness and accuracy of IRSTD.

9. Memory Guidance: In 2024, Duan et al. (217) design a Triple-domain Strategy (Tridos<sup>46</sup>) with the frequencyaware memory enhancement on spatio-temporal domain. Tridos extracts and enhances frequency features by a local-global frequency-aware module with Fourier transform. The memory enhancement captures the spatial relations of infrared targets among video frames and encodes temporal dynamics motion features via diferential learning and residual enhancing. A residual compensation reconcile possible cross-domain feature mismatches. Experiments on DAUB, ITSDT-15K and IRDST datasets show that Tridos outperforms ISNet (73), UIU-Net (155), RPCA-Net (533), ST-Trans and SSTNet (152). In 2026, Zhang et al. (518) design a unified memoryenhanced spatio-temporal fusion framework DCPNet for MISTD. First, a Deformable Spatio-Temporal Module (DSTM) captures inter-frame consistency. Second, a Content-Guided Adaptive Upsampling (CGAU) module preserves weak target structures. Third, a Prototypeaware Memory Reconstruction (PMR) module improves target-background separability. Experiments demonstrate that DCPNet outperforms 23 previous SOTA algorithms methods on pixel- and target-level metrics ofering a 5% IoU gain in complex scenes.

10. Feedback Guidance: While multi-frame detection methods exploits temporal cues, most of the methods fails in long-range dependency modeling and robustness. To address these challenges, feedback guidance has been proposed by several researchers. In 2026, Wu et al. (218) design a Dynamic Feedback Iterative Network (DFINet) which guides the model training and prediction based on the History Prediction Mask (HPMK) of previous rounds. In the training phase, DFINet extract the key features of real targets by training in multiple iterations with limited data. In the prediction phase, DFINet correct the wrong results via feedback iterative to increase the robustness. First, a Dynamic Feedback Feature Fusion Module (DFFFM) dynamically interacts HPMK with feature maps via an attention mechanism to guide feature mining and error correction. Second, a Cascaded Hybrid Pyramid Pooling Module (CHPP) allows to capture both global and local information. Third, a Dynamic Semantic Fusion Module (DSFM) employs feedback information to guide the fusion of high-level and low-level features for better feature representation in diverse scenarios.

Experiments on the NUDT-SIRST, IRSTD-1k, and SIRST datasets show that DFINet outperforms several SOTA methods. In an other work, Huang et al. (219) propose a sparse frames-based spatio-temporal semantic feedback network called FeedbackSTS-Det <sup>47</sup>. This feedback strategy with a closed-loop semantic association mechanism comprises paired forward and backward refinement modules that work cooperatively across the encoder and decoder. Both modules incorporate an embedded Sparse Semantic Module (SSM). SSM performs structured sparse temporal modeling to capture long-range dependencies with low computational cost. The overall procedure keeps a consistent training-inference pipeline allowing reliable performance transfer and increasing robustness. Experiments show that FeedbackSTS-Det outperforms model-based methods (TopHat, NRAM (384), PSTNN (383)) and data-based methods (LCNet, DNA-Net (53), RDIAN (202), ISTDU-Net (159), RPCANet (529), L2SKNet (214))

11. Noise-Suppression Guidance: In 2025, Xu et al. (534) propose a Noise-Suppression-Driven approach that adapts SAM (NSDSAM) at both internal and external levels. First, a hybrid adapter for suppressing the noise of feature maps consists of an Multilayer Perceptron (MLP) adapter and a self-attention adapter. The self-attention adapter allows entropy-aware reconstruction of features from noisy inputs and employs a gating mechanism for soft-attention fusion mitigates SAM’s sensitivity to noise. Second, a Spatial–Frequency Hybrid Module (SFHM) jointly processes spatial and frequency domains to address the self-attention model’s bias toward low-frequency components. Experiments show that that NSDSAM achieves SOTA performance.

12. Geometry Guidance: In 2025, Cai et al. (220) develop a Geometry-Aware Feature Ranking Network (Geo-RankNet) which consists of three novel modules. First, a Geometry-Aware Deformable Diference Convolution (GAD2C) learns adaptive sampling ofsets to align convolutional kernels with irregular target contours. Second, a Dynamic Feedback-Driven Top-K Attention which is a training-aware feature ranking mechanism adjusts the pooling cardinality in real time based on IoU and loss feedback. Third, a Gated Multi-Attention Fusion (GMAF) jointly exploits channel, spatial, and global context attention and uses a lightweight gating network on globally pooled features to adaptively fuse multi-scale encoder and decoder representations. Experiments on diverse SIRST based datasets show that GeoRankNet outperforms previous methods in both detection accuracy and robustness under complex backgrounds. In 2026, Gao et al. (522) design a method named RPR-Net<sup>48</sup> that bridges low-cost point supervision and mask-level detection via two components. First, a Physics-driven Adaptive Mask Generation (PAMG) module converts point annotations into compact target masks and geometric cues. Second, a lightweight Radius-aware Point Regression Network (RPR-Net) formulates IRSTD as target center localization and efective radius regression using spatiotemporal motion cues. PAMG provides pseudo masks and geometric supervision during training, while the geometric predictions of RPR-Net are fed back to PAMG for pixel-level mask recovery during inference. Experiments show that RPR-Net+PAMG outperforms ACM (37), AGPCNet (245), DNA-Net (53), ISNet (73), RDIAN (202), SCTransNet (285), DTUM (358) and SSTNet (152).

13. Ordinary Diferential Equation Guidance: Most of the fusion methods do nor efectively use global information to enforce the local features of the target. To address this problem, Xue et al. (279) design the OFSPNet<sup>49</sup> model in order to improve detection accuracy and detail-capturing ability via diversified feature representation and adaptive fusion. First, OFSPNet uses a parallel architecture integrating CNN, transformer, and Mamba—CNN. Mamba relies on its sole selection mechanism to filter out irrelevant data and keep the important features of key visual cues. Second, an ODE-inspired adaptive feature fusion module is developed as an information bottleneck to tackle high-frequency noise, while simultaneously strengthening target features via back-propagated gradients. Third, a modular design is carried out for the transformer and Mamba structures. With optimization strategies, the inference speed has been increased by 15 28% providing the dual advantages of eficient inference and high detection accuracy. In 2024, Chen and Ye (221) propose a self-attention operation to interact with frequency information and design Frequency Interaction Attention (FIA). To tackle irrelevant disturbances, a family of neural Ordinary Diferential Equation (ODE) modules are stacked behind FIA based on high-accuracy ODE solvers. A coeficient learning is employed to block design to lower the risk of gradient vanishing that most neural ODE networks have. A family of frequency neural ODE networks named FreqODEs is then obtained. Experiments on the NUAA-SIRST and IRSTD-1k datasets show the superiority of FreqODEs.

14. Fuzzy-Logic Guidance: In 2026, Zhang et al. (119) design a Fuzzy-Logic-guided Network (FLINet) which employs fuzzy sets for uncertainty-aware feature fusion. FLINet is based on a U-shaped encoder-decoder architecture and consists of two modules. First, the Global-Local Fuzzy computation module (GLF) module consists of a dual-branch fuzzy reasoning at both localwindow and global-image levels to model feature uncertainty during cross-level fusion. More specifically, the local branch increases the contrast-sensitive target cues employing an adaptive membership-based amplification. Thee global branch tackles clutter-dominant responses through global statistical normalization, jointly improving target-background separation and morphology preservation. Second, the Diference-Aware Feature fusion module DAF module increased the cross-level integration by using inter-level discrepancy maps to guide a self-attentionbased fusion. The DAF module prioritizes complementary fine-grained details from shallow layers and semantic cues from deep layers to reduce small-target information loss. Experiments on the NUDT-SIRST, NUAA-SIRST, and IRSTD-1K datasets show that FLINet ofers superior performance across multiple evaluation metrics.

15. High Resolution Guidance: In 2025, Zhang et al. (528) propose a High-resolution Guided Hierarchical Discriminative Network (H2D-Net) which consists of a High Resolution Guidance (HRG) module and a Holistic Distractor Filter (HDF). More specifically, an extra hierarchical network with fixed scale is allows to rectify the representation bias of the U-shape network through a group of the HRG modules. Thus, it facilitate bidirectional interaction between the fine-grained spatial details and multi-scale representations. Secod, the refining HDF module is embedded into the bottleneck between the encoder and decoder for the purpose of interrupting feedforward propagation of the false feature response.

## 4.8. Robust Algorithms

Table 7 shows an overview of the robust algorithms.

1. Robust Algorithms: In 2021, Hou et al. (223) propose a Robust Infrared Small Target Detection Network (RISTDnet). First, a feature extraction framework combines handcrafted feature methods and convolutional neural networks. Second, a mapping network between feature maps and the likelihood of small targets in the image is provided, and a threshold is applied on the likelihood map to obtain the targets. Experiments show that RISTDnet detect small targets with diferent sizes and low SNRs in complex backgrounds with better robustness than previous algorithms. In 2026, Zhang et al. (535) design a Selective Attention-based Network (SANet<sup>50</sup>) based on the U-Net framework with two modules. First, a Dualpath Semantic-aware Module (DSM) employs standard convolutions for local spatial detail preservation with pinwheel-shaped convolutions for expanded, directionsensitive receptive fields, followed by a Convolutional Block Attention Module (CBAM) for fine-grained spatial–channel feature recalibration. Second, a Selective Attention Fusion Module (SAFM) replaces conventional static skip connections with a spatially adaptive, learnable weighting mechanism to perform context-aware, crossscale feature fusion. Experiments the NUAA-SIRST, IRSTD-1K, and NUDT-SIRST datasets show that SANet consistently outperforms DNA-Net (53), UIU-Net (155), SCTransNet (285), and L2SKNet (214).
