In the context of multi-frame infrared small target detection, Gao et al. (406) design a robust baseline networks named (MISTNet<sup>51</sup>). based on the Information Bottleneck theory. First, a shifted neighborhood compensation block models multi-scale correspondences for implicit motion compensation to handle irregular and fast motion. Second, a progressive distillation decoder hierarchically filter out redundancy while keeping target-relevant information. Extensive experiments on the MIST dataset show that MISTNet outperforms 31 previous methods with an 6% gain in the IoU metric.

2. Sparsity Algorithms: Most of the pruning methods mainly focus on the optimization of classification networks, with an emphasis on semantic information. But, the spatial details are excessively pruned in the shallow layers causing a significant degradation of detection performance. Based on the sparse distribution of small targets in infrared images, Wu et al. (225) design in 2025 a Sparsity-Aware Global Channel Pruning (SAGCP) method. More specifically, sparse modeling allows to code the target region for the first time, and sparse priors can be induced into the feature map for the identification of redundant channels. This pruning method exploits the inherent properties of infrared small targets to extract more robust features and obtain more compact models. Applying SAGCP to previous infrared small-target detection methods, the pruned version provides superiority in model eficiency and detection performance. When applying SAGCP to DNA-NeSAGCPt, the pruned model provides a 72<sub>.</sub>34% reduction in parameters, and a 57<sub>.</sub>49% gain in floating point operations with a 2 02% increase in intersection over union (IoU). To address the sparse data, the standard “dense” implementation of traditional convolutions is ineficient. To address this limitation, Wu et al. (226) design an Adaptive Sparse Convolutional Network (ACSNet) with diferentiable binarization. First, a diferentiable binarization module adaptively learns target and background region features. Second, the features are divided into significant and redundant parts. Third, sparse convolution are employed for lightweight feature extraction.

3. Aliasing Rectification Algorithms: Most of the deep neural networks based approaches focus on extracting spatial patterns of small targets. Nevertheless, these approaches often sufer from computational redundancy and overfitting. In addition, aliasing occurs when the sampling frequency doesn’t meet the Nyquist criterion and significantly reduces the accuracy of detection. To address these challenges, Zhang et al. (228) design in 2025 an anti-aliasing network (EAA-Net) which consists of an Anti-Aliasing Contextual Aggregation (AACA) module and a Partial Diferential Channel Pruning (PDCP) module. The AACA module allows to tackle aliasing efects by filtering out channels severely afected by aliasing. The PDCP module allows tackle redundant parameters and low model eficiency. PDCP represents the weight matrix with a Partial Diferential Equation (PDE). These modules improve the capacity of anti-aliasing and reduce its size. Experiments on the IRSTD-1k and SIRST datasets show that EAA-Net outperforms SOTA methods. In 2026, Peng et al. (227) analyze the frequency-domain characteristics of small targets to improve the model’s ability to identify their critical features. As the downsampling process may introduce aliasing that distorts the target signals and results in inaccurate segmentation masks, Peng et al. (227) develop a Frequency Guidance and Aliasing Rectification Network (FGARNet). It employs a Bidirectional

Frequency Filter (BFF) to isolate specific frequency bands to improve the saliency of small targets in the frequency domain. A Wavelet Rectifying Downsampling (WRD) module allows to correct aliasing using wavelet-derived high-frequency features. Experiments on the IRSTD-1k dataset show that FGARNet outperforms the second-best by 2 74%.

4. Low Illumination Algorithms: IRSD sufers from the weak illumination and low-resolution problems. In 2020, Chen et al. (80) design a CNN model to tackle weak il lumination in natural environments. First, the Nv-Net net work allows to segment the orignal images with an en forcement layer in the front end of the framework. Sec ond, a weighted-sigmoid-cross-entropy loss function com putes the error between the prediction of the network and the ground-truth. Third, a mean-variance normalization preprocessing is employed to reduce computation time. Experiments on the LII dataset (80) show that Nv-Net method provides the best detection performance in the low illumination environment. In 2023, Chen et al. (230) provide a Fluid Dynamics-Inspired Network (FDI-Net). Based on the Taylor Central Diference (TCD) method, a TCD feature extraction block is employed in which convolution and Transformer structures are fused for local and global information. The pixel motion equation is de rived from the Navier–Stokes (N-S) equation obtaining a N-S Refinement Module which refines extracted fea tures with edge details. Thus, the TCD feature extraction block allows to determine the primary movement direction of pixels during detection. The NS Refinement Mod ule corrects some skewed directions of the pixel stream to supplement the edge details. Experiments on IRSTD-1k and SIRST datasets show that FDI-Net outperform previous SOTA methods in the presence of low illumination conditions.In 2025, Zhang et al. (229) develop a Computational Fluid Dynamic Network (CFD-Net) derived from computational fluid dynamics. Based on the unilateral diference equation with third-order, a unilateral diferential residual structure is employed as the back bone. Thus, the pixel stream only flows in the forward direction. Then, a Switch-Controlled Multidirectional Treatment Tank (SMTT) allows to dynamically guide the pixel stream to the appropriate path for diferent targets with varying shapes and orientation. Experiments on the IRSTD-1k and SIRST datasets show that CFD-Net out perform previous SOTA methods in the low illumination environments.

## 4.9. Lightweight Algorithms

Diferent strategies have been developed to obtain lightweight networks. In 2019, Zhao et al. (232) propose a lightweight CNN named TBC-Net. TBCNet comprises a Target Extraction Module (TEM) and a Semantic Constraint Module (SCM). A joint loss function and a training method are also developed. The SCM module provides a semantic constraint on TEM by combining the high-level classification task and addresses the dificulty to learn features due to the class imbalance problem. During the training phase, the targets are extracted and are further classified by the SCM module. During the inference phase, the TEM module is solely employed to detect the small targets. Experiments show that TBC-Net reduces the false alarm due to complex backgrounds. BC-Net can reach real-time detection on the NVIDIA Jetson AGX development board making it suitable for deployment in real conditions. In 2024, Yu et al. (541) design a Lightweight and Robust network (LR-Net) without complex structure. LR-Net ofers an efective balance between detection accuracy and resource requirements. More specifically, a Lightweight Feature Extraction Attention (LFEA) module extracts target features and strengthens information interaction across channels. A Refined Feature Transfer (RFT) module improves the network’s feature refinement extraction ability with little resource requirements. A Low-level Feature Distribution (LFD) strategy allows to address the problem of small target loss in high-level feature maps. An Simplified Bilinear interpolation Attention Module (SBAM) promotes the guidance constraints of low-level features on high-level features and the fusion of the two. The traditional resizing method is replaced by a training and inference cropping strategy. Experimental results show that LR-Net achieves SOTA performance and is ranked at the 3rd place in the "ICPR 2024 Resource-Limited Infrared Small Target Detection Challenge Track 2: Lightweight Infrared Small Target Detection".

In 2023, Chen et al. (548) develop a lightweight network with high-speed inference and a knowledge distillation method for learning higher-order semantic information from the transformer network. First, Chen et al. (548) design each stage in the detection network, named Multi-scale Dilated Pyramid Network (MDPNet). This design enlarges the receptive field and improves the ability to extract contextual features. Second, a knowledge distillation allows to bridge the detection performance gap with the transformer. Based on the self-attention mechanism, a semantic distillation sub-network is constructed between the teacher transformer and student convolution network. Experiments on the SIRST and NUDT-SIRST datasets show that MDPNet outperforms previous networks requiring a number of parameters of only 0.23 M. MDPNet achieves an inference speed of 137 FPS for 320 × 320 infrared images.

In 2025, Li et al. (233) develop an Infrared Low-level Network (ILNet<sup>52</sup>) that considers infrared small targets as salient regions characterized by semantic information. More specifically, ILNet employs low-level information instead of treating it uniformly with high-level information. A lightweight feature fusion module, named the Interactive Polarized Orthogonal Fusion module (IPOF), allows to integrate important low-level features from the shallow layers into the deep layers. A dynamic 1-D aggregation layers are inserted into the IPOF allowing to dynamically adjust the aggregation of low-dimensional information according to the number of input channels. In an other work, Jin et al. (140) design a lightweight network based on an improved YOLOv10 (IR-WSANet) increasing the detection performance via a frequency-spatial joint optimization strategy. First, a discrete wavelet transform convolution (DWaveletConv) is employed into the backbone network. The fusion of high-frequency details and low-frequency semantics is improved by multi band feature decomposition to suppress noise interference Second, a cooperative module (POS-SHSA) improves the positioning accuracy of small targets. Experiments on the SIDD and HIT-UAV datasets show that IR-WSANet improves the detection performance in complex scenes through the combination of frequency domain filtering enhancement and space-channel dual attention mechanism. Duan et al. (143) design a lightweight architecture based on RT-DETR named RT-DETR-CST. First, a Cross-Channel Feature Attention Network (CFAN) allows to achieve channel-weighted feature fusion via residual connections to suppress invalid background channels. Second, a Scale-Wise Feature Network (SWN) employs depthwise separable convolutions and stochastic depth for multi-scale feature extraction. Third, a Texture/Detail Capture Network (TCN) achieves edge/detail capture via linear decomposition. Experiments on the ISDD datasets show that RT-DETR-CST ofers real-time performance in infrared ship remote sensing small-target detection. In an other work, Li et al. (545) investigate the most essential information from a more crucial domain. A theoretical analysis demonstrate that the global temporal saliency and correlation information in the temporal profile ofer significant superiority in separating target signals from other signals. First, Li et al. (545) construct the first prediction attribution tool and verify the importance of the temporal profile information. S, the IRST detection task is formulated as a one-dimensional signal anomaly detection task. Thus, Li et al. (545) propose an eficient Deep temporal Probe network (DeepPro<sup>53</sup>) that executes only calculations in the time dimension. Experiments show that DeepPro outperforms existing state-of-the-art IRST detection methods with reasonable computation time. Zang et al. (84) propose a lightweight model—one named LMDNet with few parameters. LMDNet reduces computational load—while surpassing the average detection accuracy and IoU values of previous SOTA models. First, a spatial-channel diference convolution module allows to extract spatial and channel features while maintaining low computational requirement. Second, a global feature enhancement Mamba layer accelerates target localization and ofers precise detection in complex backgrounds. Third, a self-learning joint loss function dynamically adjusts weights during training to optimize the contributions of diferent loss components. Practically, the FPS is several times higher than that of larger model-based methods, with Params and FLOPs of only 0.024M and 0.168G, respectively. Its overall performance outperforms previous lightweight methods.

In 2026, Wang et al. (107) provide a Lightweight Local Con trast and Edge Fusion Approach (LLCEFA). LLCEFA consists of a dual-branch architecture with a main branch and an edge branch designed to take into account the synergistic advantages of prior knowledge guidance and data-driven learning. About the main branch, a Local Contrast Attention Fusion (LCAF) module employs the local contrast prior to obtain the input image into more discriminative feature representations. An edge branch employs an Adaptive Edge Weight Extraction (AEWE) module to independently derive edge weights for enhancing intermediate-layer features. This design decouples prior knowledge processing from the backbone’s architecture making LLCEFA suitable to be a plug-and-play enhancer compatible with various backbone methods, achieving a performance boost with generating a minimal additional burden. In an other work, Zhang et al. (542) propose a Tropical-Fish Swarm Pruning for IR (TFSP-IR). It is a compact architecture that employs feature restoration, adaptive filtering, and bioinspired pruning. First, a Fine-detail-guided Multilevel Feature Compensation (F-MFC) mechanism aggregates information from adjacent layers. Thus, small-target details persist into the network’s deeper stages. Second, a Cross-level Feature Correlation (CFC) module employs an energy-based filter from high-level, noise-reduced representations. It allows to suppress clutter and enhancing target signal. Third, an Eficient Fast Channel Pruning (EFCP) strategy adjusts pruning depth and breadth according to the evolving spatial relationships within feature maps. Experiments on three datasets show that TFSP-IR ofers state-of-the-art performance in both accuracy and eficiency. Zhao et al. (543) propose a lightweight Shape-Texture Mutual-Guided Predictor-Corrector Network (STMG-PCNet). STMG-PCNet ofers a balance between accuracy and eficiency by providing a collaborative design of a detection branch and a shape-aware branch. First, a progressive lightweight dilated dense module in the detection branch employs dense connections and cascaded multiscale dilated convolutions to improve local texture retention and precise localization. Second, a shape-aware vision Transformer module in the shape-aware branch allows to model target edges and shape features via global context interaction. Third, a numerical predictor-corrector fusion module achieves lightweight multistage feature fusion. Experiments show that STMG-PCNet outperforms previous algorithms in detection accuracy and computational eficiency. In an other work, Zhang et al. (542) propose a Tropical-Fish-Swarm Pruning for IR (TFSP-IR). It is a compact architecture that employs feature restoration, adaptive filtering, and bioinspired pruning. First, a Fine-detail-guided Multilevel Feature Compensation (F-MFC) mechanism aggregates information from adjacent layers. Thus, small-target details persist into the network’s deeper stages. Second, a Cross-level Feature Correlation (CFC) module employs an energy-based filter from high-level, noise-reduced representations. It allows to suppress clutter and enhancing target signal. Third, an Eficient Fast Channel Pruning (EFCP) strategy adjusts pruning depth and breadth according to the evolving spatial relationships within feature maps. Experiments on three datasets show that TFSP-IR ofers state-of-the-art performance in both accuracy and eficiency. Zhao et al. (543) propose a lightweight Shape-Texture Mutual-Guided Predictor-Corrector Network (STMG-PCNet). STMG-PCNet ofers a balance between accuracy and eficiency by providing a collaborative design of a detection branch and a shape-aware branch. First, a progressive lightweight dilated dense module in the detection branch employs dense connections and cascaded multiscale dilated convolutions to improve local texture retention and precise localization. Second, a shape-aware vision Transformer module in the shape-aware branch allows to model target edges and shape features via global context interaction. Third, a numerical predictor-corrector fusion module achieves lightweight multistage feature fusion. Experiments show that STMG-PCNet outperforms previous algorithms in detection accuracy and, computational eficiency. In an other work, Cui et al. (544) build upon the traditional U-Net architecture a lightweight Adaptive Memory Fusion (AMF) module at the deepest layer of the network. This module allows to take into account the strength of attention mechanisms in capturing long-range contextual information and adaptively fuses multi-frame inputs to improve feature representations. Experiments show that UAMF<sup>54</sup> allows to reduce computational complexity by two orders of magnitude compared with previous SOTA models. In 2026, Lu et al. (546) develop an architecture named WSNet which contains only 0.054 M parameters and requires 1.050 G FLOPs. WSNet was the most lightweight model in the field achieving the fastest inference speed to date.

Binarized Neural Networks (BNNs) ofers eficiency in model compression. Thus, Xin et al. (547) design a Binarized Infrared Small-Target Detection Network (BiisNet<sup>55</sup>). It keeps the core operations of binarized convolutions while integrating fullprecision features into the network’s information flow. More specifically, a Dot Binary Convolution retains fine-grained semantic information in feature maps while still exploiting the binarized convolution operations. A smooth and adaptive Dynamic Softsign function ofers a more comprehensive and progressively finer gradient during backpropagation improving model stability and promoting an optimal weight distribution. Experiments show that BiisNet outperforms other binary architectures and has strong competitiveness among state-of-the-art full-precision models.

In summary, model compression is the main way for obtaining lightweight networks and structured pruning is a representative lightweight method (552). Due to the extreme characteristics of infrared small targets (e.g., weak texture, shape, and color distribution), directly applying existing pruning methods to infrared small target detection appears challenging because they have been designed for other tasks (552) reaching to the following weaknesses as developed in Li et al. (553):

• Error Pruning of Essentialrr Modules: Existing pruning methods consider the specially designed modules (e.g.,

CBAM in DNA-Net (53) , BLAM in ALCNet (312)) and conventional components as equally important. Several essential modules are thus removed generating decrease in performance .

• Structural Coupled Dependencies: Advanced infrared small target detection networks use multi-scale feature fusion or residual cross-layer connections to obtain strong inter-channel dependencies. But, existing pruning methods are insensitive to these coupled relationships and rely on expert-driven grouping strategies, which struggle to adapt to parameter-sharing mechanisms in cross-layer connections and generate performance degradation.

• Weight Correlation Constraint: Existing pruning methods focus on the identification redundant structures while ignoring the substructures correlation. This fact generates feature distribution perturbation and weaken the recovery and generalization ability

To address these three challenges, Li et al. (553) design a Structure Grouping collaborative Decorrelated L2-norm Prun ing (SGDP<sup>56</sup>) which is a task-aware structured pruning method specifically designed to infrared small target detection. SGDP method is not intended to reduce Params and FLOPs, but to achieve a balance between model compression and small target detection performance. First, a Shield Layer (SL) strategy protects important layers from pruning. Second, a Hierarchical Coupling Structure Grouping (HCSG) strategy exploits hierarchical coupling relationships and input–output dependencies to partition non-shielded structures into grouped substructures. HCSG avoids the disruption of critical information flow paths of small targets and ensures networks regularity. Third, a Decorrelated L2-norm Regularization (DL2R) strategy enforces substructure regularization and transform correlation matrices into non-diagonally dominant forms. The redundant weights are suppressed and these features are redistributed to the retained filters. Thus, the layer-wise optimization with regularized weight-bias updating improves pruned model performance.

## 5. Wavelets based Methods

Table 8 shows an overview of the wavelets based methods. In 2021, Song et al. (281) propose a semi-supervised network based on Haar Wavelet (HaarConv) using YOLOv5-s. The HaarConv module based on the high-frequency features is obtained by Haar wavelet decomposition. A HaarUp-HaarDown module is obtained by using Haar forward and inverse transform,replacing the up-sampling layer of the feature pyramid network and the down-sampling layer of backbone network to preserve smaller target features. A pseudo-label-based module conducts semi-supervised learning and reduces the labeling cost. Experiments show that HaarConv improve the detection accuracy of dim and small infrared ship targets by more than

![](images/8e11c24a0154f5205d13b12f7ab67db1b7688c982ffb07914b181df88eaa5b66.jpg)  
Table 8: Wavelets Methods: An Overview (24 papers).

2%, and the number of parameters increases by only about   
0.02M.

In 2024, He et al. (559) design a Wavelet Transform-based Augmented Perception Network (WTAPNet<sup>57</sup>). First, an Enhancement and Enlarge (EE) module enhances the perceptual capability by magnifying image resolution and augmenting target features at the same time. Second, a Discrete Wavelet Transform-based Downsampling (DWTD) module and an inverse Wavelet Transform-based Fusion (IWTF) module collaboratively work to extract and combine multiscale features. Third, a bottom-up path fusion strategy highlight and preserve small-target features. Experiments on the NUDT-SIRST and the SIRST datasets show that WTAPNet obtains similar performance than previous SOTA algorithms. Zhang et al. (318) propose a Wavelet Structure-Regularized Soft Channel Pruning Network (IRPruneDet<sup>58</sup>). IRPruneDet represents the weight matrix in the wavelet domain and formulate a wavelet channel pruning strategy. A wavelet regularization induces structural sparsity without incurring extra memory usage. A soft channel reconstruction method keeps key target information against premature pruning. Extensive experiments demonstrate that IRPruneDet provides a 64 13% reduction in parameters and a 51 19% decrease in FLOPS with an improvement of IoU from 73 31% to 75 12% and nIoU from 70 92% to 74 30%.

In 2025, Zhang et al. (554) propose a Wavelet Mamba with Reversible Structure Network (WMRNet) which comprises a Discrete Wavelet Mamba (DW-Mamba) module and a Third-order Diference Equation guided Reversible (TDE-Rev) structure. First, DW-Mamba uses the Discrete Wavelet Transform to decompose images into multiple subbands, integrating this information into the state equations of a state space model. DW- Mamba minimizes frequency interference keeping a global perspective avoiding background aliasing. Second, TDE-Rev suppresses edge aliasing efects by refining the target edges and promotes feature interactions via a reversible structure. Experiments on the IRSTD-1k and SIRST datasets show that WMRNet outperforms previous SOTA algorithms. Li et al. (555) propose a Feature Synthesis with wavelet Coeficients Networks (FSCNet) addressing the challenge of information loss prevalent deep learning models during the down-sampling process. First, FSCNet employs a wavelet coeficient-based feature extraction mechanism to separate small targets from the background. Based on a Feature Attention Gate Module (FAGM), the network enhances high-frequency details. Second, a Group Spatial-Channel Transformer Block (G-SCTB) allows the interaction and enhancement of multi-scale features. Experiments on the NUAA-SIRST, NUDT-SIRST and IRSTD-1K datasets show that FSCNet provides competitive results compared to ACM (37), ALCNet (312), ISNet (73), ISTDU-Net, RDIAN (202), DNA-Net (53), UIU-Net (155) and SCTransNet (285). Ma et al. (558) propose a Wavelet Steered network (WaveTD<sup>59</sup>) by combining Discrete Wavelet Transform (DWT) into CNN. First, Haar DWT and inverse DWT (IDWT) are embedded for lossless downsampling and upsampling for more efective feature extraction within the CNN. Second, a hybrid attention mechanism named Wavelet Steered Transformer (WST) enhances the DWT features both spatially and across channels. WST consists of two key components: 1) a Channel-wise Transformer which adapts a channel-wise Transformer to improve semantic information and suppress background clutter and noise, and 2) a Dilated-Gate Convolutional Module which enhances spatial location accuracy. It uses a combination of diferent kernel sizes and dilation rates to improve spatial accuracy. Experiments show that WaveTD outperforms MSHNet (40), WTAPNet (559), ABCNet, AGPCNet (245), DNA-Net (53), LIG (572), PSTNN (383), TLLCM and TopHat.

In 2025, Jing et al. (120) propose a Synergistic Wavelet-Attention Network (SWAN) to perceive targets from both spatial and frequency domains. First, SWAN exploits a Haar Wavelet Convolution (HWConv) for a deep, cross-domain fusion of the frequency energy and spatial details of small target. Second, a Shifted Spatial Attention (SSA) mechanism models long-range spatial dependencies with linear computational complexity for contextual awareness. Third, a Residual Dual-Channel Attention (RDCA) module adaptively calibrates channel-wise feature responses to avoird background interference while amplifying target-pertinent signals. Experiments show that SWAN outperforms ACM (37) (37), ALCNet (312), DNA-Net (53), RDIAN (202), UIU-Net (155), SCTransNet (285) and DATransNet. Jiang et al. (560) propose a Wavelet-Recalibrated Semi-Supervised Network (WRSSNet) that employs synthetic data augmentation, feature reconstruction, and semi-supervised learning to exploit the potential of unlabeled infrared images under limited supervision. First, a dataset containing 843 visible-light small target images employs an improved CycleGAN model to convert them into high-quality pseudo-infrared images. Second, a lightweight Wavelet-Enhanced Channel Recalibration and Fusion (WECRF) module employs wavelet decomposition with both channel and spatial attention mechanisms. WECRF allows adaptive reweighting and fusion of multi-scale features, highlighting high-frequency details and weak target responses. Experiments on the NUAA-SIRST and IRSTD-1K datasets show that WRSSNet provides better performance than DNA-Net (53) and UIU-Net (155). Li et al. (4) develop a doubly wavelet-guided Invariance learning framework (Ivan-ISTD<sup>60</sup>). First, training samples aligned with the target domain are generated using Wavelet-guided Cross-domain Synthesis. This wavelet-guided alignment module separates the target background via multi-frequency wavelet filtering. Second, a Real-domain Noise Invariance Learning (RNIL) module extracts real noise characteristics from the target domain to obtain a dynamic noise library. Thus, RNIL learns noise invariance via self-supervised loss. Experiments show that Ivan-ISTD outperforms most of the time ACM-Net, ALCNet (312),DNA-Net (53), RDIAN (202), ISTDU-Net,UIU-Net (155), HCF-Net and SCTransNet (285) in presence of diverse noise distributions.

In 2025, Cui et al. (562) design an Adaptive Receptive Field Convolution and Wavelet-Attentive Hierarchical Network (ARFC-WAHNet<sup>61</sup>). It comprises a Multi-Receptive Field Feature Interaction Convolution (MRFFIConv) module to adaptively extract discriminative features by integrating multiple convolutional branches with a gated unit. Second, a Wavelet Frequency Enhancement Downsampling (WFED) module exploits Haar wavelet transform and frequency-domain reconstruction to improve target features and suppress background noise. Third, a High-Low Feature Fusion (HLFF) module integrates low-level details with high-level semantics. Finally, a Global Median Enhancement Attention (GMEA) enhances feature diversity and expressiveness via global attention. Experiments on the SIRST, NUDT-SIRST, and IRSTD-1k datasts show that ARFC-WAHNet outperforms most of the time model-driven models (IPI, PSTNN) and data-driven models (ACM (37), ALCNet (312), DNA-Net (53), RDIAN (202), UIU-Net (155), AGPCNet (245), MSHNet (40), MDIGCNet (573)).

In 2025, Zhang et al. (561) propose a wavelet structureregularized multidimensional musical scale Soft Channel Pruning (SCP) method named IRPruneDeXt<sup>62</sup>. It represents the weight matrix in the wavelet domain and formulates a Wavelet Channel Pruning (WCP) strategy. A wavelet regularization induces structural sparsity without incurring extra memory usage. A Multidimensional Musical scale Soft Channel Reconstruction (MMSCR) module adapts the strategy across temporal and spatial dimensions to keep key target information and prevent premature pruning. MMSCR balances pruning and reconstruction via a musical scale feedback efect. Extensive experiments demonstrate that IRPruneDeXt outperforms previous SOTA methods with a 65 68% reduction in parameters, a 51 77% decrease in floating-point operations (FLOPs), an augmentation of over union (IoU) from 73 31% to 76 17% and of normalized IoU (nIoU) from 70 92% to 75 08%. Li et al. (564) develop a Wavelet Transform Convolution and Hierarchical Multi-Scale Feature Fusion Network (WT-HMFF) which consists of a Wavelet Transform Convolution (WTConv) module and a Hierarchical Multi-Scale Feature Fusion (HMFF) module. First, WTConv expands the receptive field through wavelet convolution to capture global contextual information while keeping target shape characteristics. Second HMFF allows the fusion of shallow and deep features keeping the high resolution of deep feature maps and avoiding the disappearance of small target features. Experiments on the SIRST and IRSTD-1k datasets demonstrate that WT-HMFF provides most of the time better results than MDvsFA (243), ACM (37), DNA-Net (53), Pconv (7) (7), ALCNet (312), IRPruneDet (318), UIU-Net (155), and SCTransNet (285).

In 2026, Fan et al. (282) propose a HaarTransNet<sup>63</sup> method which exploits frequency-domain decoupling and target–background relative saliency modeling. A Haar Decoupled Downsampling Block (HDDB) allows to decouple input features along the channel dimension via the Haar Wavelet Transform (WT). HDDB avoids feature confusion and alleviates small-target attenuation during downsampling. A Haar Channel Cross Transformer (HCCT) provides a channel cross-attention mechanism to model cross-level global correlations. The low-frequency background cue guides attention to dynamically model the relative salience of targets in complex backgrounds. Experiments on the NUDT-SIRST and IRSTD-1k datasets show that HaarTransNet provides robustness in challenging scenes.

In 2026, Li et al. (79) design a Wavelet-based Cross-Domain Multistage Feature Fusion Network (WCDMF-Net) that synergizes frequency-domain analysis with spatial-contextual learning. First, a parallel spatial–frequency dual-stream architecture employs Haar Wavelet Transform (HWT) and inverse Discrete Wavelet Transform (iDWT) for multiscale frequency feature extraction and reconstruction. Second, an adaptive spectral–spatial fusion block enhances features within the spatial and spectral domains and fuses these refined representations to exploit the complementary strengths of both domains. Third, a Multiscale Feature Aggregation Module (MFAM) aggregates multiscale output feature maps in the decoding phase. A multilevel cross-entropy loss function allows to achieve target localization and mask prediction. Experiments on the NUDT-SIRST, NUAA-SIRST, and IRSTD-1k datasets show that WCDMF-Net provides similar performances than SOTA methods. Cheng et al. (556) propose a state space model with Dynamic Frequency cue Aware Network (DFAwareNet<sup>64</sup>) which exploits spatial and wavelet-domain representations. A Wavelet Guided Module (WGM) refines wavelet components to improve target-related features while suppressing background interference. A Vision State Space

Model (VSSM) captures global context with linear complexity. An Hybrid Repair Gate Network (HRGN) preserves weak or subtle targets via a multi-dilated aggregation. A Mixed Feature Fusion Module (MFFM) enhances target localization through spatial–channel integration. Experiments on four benchmark datasets demonstrate that DFAwareNet achieves competitive or superior detection performance compared to TopHat, model-driven approaches (LIG (572), PSTNN (383), TLLCM (574), HBMLCM) and data-driven approaches (WTAPNet (559), MSHNet (40), MTU-Net (284), AGPCNet (245), DNA-Net (53)). Zhang et al. (557) develop a Stride-free Dual-domain Adaptive Network (SDANet) using a U-shaped architecture. First, a High-frequency Modulated Downsampling (HMD) performs stride-free downsampling by fusing the low-frequency band and the modulated high-frequency subbands generated from the discrete wavelet transform. HMD provides richer target details during downsampling while suppressing background clutter interference. Second, a Stride-free Wavelet Enhancement Module (SWEM) employs a Multi-scale Wavelet Convolution (MWC) to extract spatial and overlooked frequency-domain representations. In the decoder, an Attention-Routed Scale Adaptive (ARSA) module dynamically combies local and non-local features via input-dependent attention routing to enhance scale robustness. Experiments show that SDANet achieves superior detection accuracy and robustness compared with previous SOTA methods.

In 2026, Lian et al. (26) design a Parametric Manifold Network (PMNet<sup>65</sup>) which comprises frequency-domain analysis with manifold learning. First, PMNet separates high-frequency features from the spatial domain to encode high-frequency target details across channels; accordingly. PMNet preserves the geometric structure of the target while suppressing noise. Second, a low-frequency feature block refines structural features improving discrimination between targets and backgrounds. Extensive experiments show the PMNet outperforms previous SOTA methods in both accuracy and robustness demonstrating the efectiveness of integrating wavelet decomposition with manifold learning. Ding et al. (563) develop a Kolmogorov-Arnold Network (IRKAN) with fixed, node-based activations with learnable functions on the network edges. IRKAN learns optimal, data-driven transformations for complex infrared signals. Wavelet basis functions are used to parameterize these activations. It introduces a signal-processing prior into the network exploiting the time-frequency localization properties of wavelets to function as a learnable matched filter. Experiments on the WideIRSTD-Full dataset show that IRKAN achieves a mean normalized Intersection over Union of 68 89% and a low False Alarm Rate of 8 33 × 10<sup>6</sup>, with computational eficiency requiring 3.96M parameters. Zero-shot evaluations on the unseen NUDT-SIRSTD and IRSTD-1K datasets demonstrate that IRKAN ofers cross-resolution generalization capabilities.

## 6. Mamba-based State Space Models

Mamba is a State Space Model (SSM)-based neural network architecture designed for linear-time sequence modeling. However, the direct application of Mamba alone is not efective, as it cannot fully exploit local features (10). Thus, hybrid approaches have been developed. Table 9 shows an overview of the existing methods based on a Mamba architecture.

In 2024, Chen et al. (578) develop a nested structure Mambain-Mamba (MiM-ISTD) which comprises Outer and Inner Mamba blocks to capture both global and local features. Local patches are considered as “visual sentences” and the Outer Mamba explores the global information. Each visual sentence is then decomposed into sub-patches as “visual words”. The Inner Mamba explores the local information among words in the visual sentence with low computational costs. By using both the visual word and visual sentence features, MiM-ISTD explores both global and local information. Experiments on the NUAA-SIRST and IRSTD-1k datasets show that MiM-ISTD is 7times faster than several SOTA methods and reduces GPU memory usage by 62 2% on 2048 × 2048 images.

In 2024, in the context of infrared dense small target detection, Xu et al. (76) develop a Fuzzy-Aware Nested Mamba (FANM) network. It leverages shallow, middle, and deep Mamba networks to fully exploit and integrate local and global multi-scale features. The extracted features are then passed to a Feature Pyramid Network (FPN) to balance the spatial resolution and semantic information of each feature map layer. After, the output features at each level are fed into the detection head. It comprises two branches: one for regressing the positions of arbitrarily distributed bounding boxes, and the other one for predicting the quality of the bounding boxes. The proposed fuzzy perceptual loss function allows to optimize these branches. Experiments on the DMIST dataset show that FANM outperforms several previous algorithms in the case of dense scenarios.

In 2025, Li et al. (86) propose a Hybrid Mamba–CNN UNet (HMCNet). Its hybrid architecture extracts the local features and model the global context. Experiments on the IRSTD-1k and MISTD (86) datasets show that HMCNet outperforms RLCM, MPCM, PSTNN (383) and RDIAN (202) with high-speed inference capabilities. Lu and Zhang (10) integrate a visual Mamba module into a lightweight CNN-based network which combines the ability of CNNs to model local features and a visual Mamba module captures long-distance global features. Therefore, this model extracts both local and long-distance global feature information. Experiments on the NUAA-SIRST and NUAA-SIRSTv2 datasets show that this model provides better accuracy and lower computational complexity.

In 2025, Yu et al. (583) propose a Spatial-BiDirectional Mamba Network (SBMambaNet) which exploits Spatial-BiDirectional Mamba Blocks (SBMBs) working in parallel with Skip Connections. SBMambaNe comprises two components. First, aSpatial-BiDirectional Cross Mamba (SCM) captures local spatial information in sequences and enhances the exchange between local spatial features and global channel data. It models bidirectional sequences for global context, and fuses features via gated mechanisms to strengthen long-range dependency and information flow. Second, a Mixed-Scale Spatial-Gate Feed-forward Network (MSGFN) improves feature discriminability via a multi-scale strategy and crossspace-channel information interaction. Second, a CNN-Mamba Channel-wise Fusion module (CMCFusion) guide the fusion of multi-scale channel information and pixel-level spatial relationships at the decoder stage. Experiments show that SB-MambaNet outperforms most of the time model-based methods (PSTNN (383), TLLCM (574), NRAM (384), MSLSTIPT (431), IPI, MLCL-Net (407), data-based methods (MDvsFA (243), LSPM, ACM (37), ISNet (73), GGL-Net, DNA-Net (53), AGPCNet (245)), three hybrid algorithms combining CNN and ViT (IAANet (378), Rkformer (610), FATCNet) and MiM-ISTD.

(615)), LPASU-SwinDet [Swin Transformer] (Ma et al. (616))</td></tr><tr><td rowspan="3"></td></tr><tr><td>Qformer (Yu et al. (617)), DifTransNet (Hu et al. (618)), CL-STFormer (Ma et al.
